</td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background:lavender">Personal details</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data"><span style="display:none">(<span class="bday">1764-03-13</span>)</span>13 March 1764<br /><a href="/wiki/Fallodon" title="Fallodon">Fallodon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northumberland" title="Northumberland">Northumberland</a>, England</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">17 July 1845<span style="display:none">(1845-07-17)</span> (aged&#160;81)<br /><a href="/wiki/Howick,_Northumberland" title="Howick, Northumberland">Howick, Northumberland</a>, England</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Political party</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Whiggism" title="Whiggism">Whig</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1151524712">.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin2px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-2px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-line-margin3px{line-height:0;margin-bottom:-3px}.mw-parser-output .marriage-display-ws{display:inline;white-space:nowrap}</style>
<div class="marriage-display-ws"><div style="display:inline-block;line-height:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Mary_Grey,_Countess_Grey" title="Mary Grey, Countess Grey">Mary Ponsonby</a></div>&#32;<div (British Army officer)">Charles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Frederick_Grey" title="Frederick Grey">Frederick</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Eliza_Courtney" title="Eliza Courtney">Eliza Courtney</a> (illegitimate)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Parent</th><td class="infobox-data"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1126788409">.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul{line-height:inherit;list-style:none;margin:0;padding:0}.mw-parser-output .plainlist ol li,.mw-parser-output .plainlist ul li{margin-bottom:0}</style><div class="plainlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Grey,_1st_Earl_Grey" title="Charles Grey, 1st Earl Grey">Charles Grey, 1st Earl Grey</a> (father)</li></ul></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Relatives</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/House_of_Grey" title="House of Grey">House of Grey</a> (family)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Alma_mater" title="Alma mater">Alma mater</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Trinity College, Cambridge</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Signature</th><td class="infobox-data"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey_Signature.svg" class="mw-file-description" title="Charles Grey, 2nd Earl Grey&#39;s signature"><img alt="Grey." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey_Signature.svg/128px-Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey_Signature.svg.png" decoding="async" width="128" height="62" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey_Signature.svg/192px-Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey_Signature.svg.png 1.5x, /></a></span></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Charles Grey, 2nd Earl Grey</b> <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:85%; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Knight_of_the_Order_of_the_Garter" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight of the Order of the Garter">KG</a>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">PC</a></span> (13 March 1764&#160;– 17 July 1845), known as <b>Viscount Howick</b> between 1806 and 1807, was a British <a href="/wiki/Whiggism" title="Whiggism">Whig</a> politician who served as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">Prime Minister of the United Kingdom</a> from 1830 to 1834. He was a descendent of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Grey" title="House of Grey">House of Grey</a> and the namesake of <a href="/wiki/Earl_Grey_tea" title="Earl Grey tea">Earl Grey tea</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Grey was a long-time leader of multiple <a href="/wiki/Reform_movement" title="Reform movement">reform movements</a>, and during his time as prime minister his government brought about two notable reforms. The <a href="/wiki/Reform_Act_1832" title="Reform Act 1832">Reform Act 1832</a> enacted <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_reform" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliamentary reform">parliamentary reform</a>, greatly increasing the electorate of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="House of Commons of the United Kingdom">House of Commons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Strangic_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Strangic-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Slavery_Abolition_Act_1833" title="Slavery Abolition Act 1833">Slavery Abolition Act 1833</a> led to the <a href="/wiki/Abolitionism_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Abolitionism in the United Kingdom">abolition of slavery in most of the British Empire</a>, with compensation to be paid to slave-owners. Grey was a strong opponent of the foreign and domestic policies of <a href="/wiki/William_Pitt_the_Younger" title="William Pitt the Younger">William Pitt the Younger</a> in the 1790s. In 1807, he resigned as <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Secretary" title="Foreign Secretary">foreign secretary</a> to protest against <a href="/wiki/George_III" title="George III">George III</a>'s uncompromising rejection of <a href="/wiki/Catholic_emancipation" title="Catholic emancipation">Catholic emancipation</a>. Grey finally resigned as prime minister in 1834 over disagreements in his cabinet regarding Ireland, and retired from politics. Scholars rank him highly among British prime ministers, believing that he averted much civil strife and enabled <a href="/wiki/Victorian_era" title="Victorian era">Victorian</a> progress.<sup id="cite_ref-Strangic_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Strangic-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey,_KG,_PC.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png/170px-Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png/255px-Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png/340px-Shield_of_arms_of_Charles_Grey%2C_2nd_Earl_Grey%2C_KG%2C_PC.png 2x" data-file-width="1158" data-file-height="1158" /></a><figcaption>Shield of arms of Charles Grey, 2nd Earl Grey</figcaption></figure>
<p>Descended from a long-established Northumbrian family seated at <a href="/wiki/Howick_Hall" title="Howick Hall">Howick Hall</a>, Grey was the second but eldest surviving son of <a href="/wiki/Charles_Grey,_1st_Earl_Grey" title="Charles Grey, 1st Earl Grey">General Charles Grey, 1st Earl Grey</a> <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Bath" title="Order of the Bath">KB</a> (1729–1807) and his wife Elizabeth (1743/4–1822), a daughter of George Grey of Southwick, County Durham. He had four brothers and two sisters. He was educated at <a href="/wiki/Richmond_School" title="Richmond School">Richmond School</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> followed by <a href="/wiki/Eton_College" title="Eton College">Eton</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Trinity College, Cambridge</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> acquiring a facility in Latin and in English composition and declamation that enabled him to become one of the foremost parliamentary orators of his generation.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_career">Political career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Political career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span id="Elected_to_Parliament.2C_1786"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Elected_to_Parliament,_1786">Elected to Parliament, 1786</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Elected to Parliament, 1786">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Grey was elected to Parliament for the <a href="/wiki/Northumberland_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Northumberland (UK Parliament constituency)">Northumberland constituency</a> on 14 September 1786, aged just 22. He became a part of the Whig circle of <a href="/wiki/Charles_James_Fox" title="Charles James Fox">Charles James Fox</a>, <a href="/wiki/Richard_Brinsley_Sheridan" title="Richard Brinsley Sheridan">Richard Brinsley Sheridan</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/George_IV" title="George IV">Prince of Wales</a>, and soon became one of the major leaders of the Whig party. He was the youngest manager on the committee for prosecuting <a href="/wiki/Warren_Hastings" title="Warren Hastings">Warren Hastings</a>. The Whig historian <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Babington_Macaulay,_1st_Baron_Macaulay" class="mw-redirect" title="Thomas Babington Macaulay, 1st Baron Macaulay">T. B. Macaulay</a> wrote in 1841:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>At an age when most of those who distinguish themselves in life are still contending for prizes and fellowships at college, he had won for himself a conspicuous place in Parliament. No advantage of fortune or connection was wanting that could set off to the height his splendid talents and his unblemished honour. At twenty-three he had been thought worthy to be ranked with the veteran statesmen who appeared as the delegates of the British Commons, at the bar of the British nobility. All who stood at that bar, save him alone, are gone, culprit, advocates, accusers. To the generation which is now in the vigour of life, he is the sole representative of a great age which has passed away. But those who, within the last ten years, have listened with delight, till the morning sun shone on the tapestries of the House of Lords, to the lofty and animated eloquence of Charles Earl Grey, are able to form some estimate of the powers of a race of men among whom he was not the foremost.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_Grey_(1764-1845),_by_Henry_Bone.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Charles_Grey_%281764-1845%29%2C_by_Henry_Bone.jpg/220px-Charles_Grey_%281764-1845%29%2C_by_Henry_Bone.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="259" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Charles_Grey_%281764-1845%29%2C_by_Henry_Bone.jpg/330px-Charles_Grey_%281764-1845%29%2C_by_Henry_Bone.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/ff/Charles_Grey_%281764-1845%29%2C_by_Henry_Bone.jpg 2x" data-file-width="359" data-file-height="422" /></a><figcaption>Grey in a blue coat, white waistcoat and tied cravat, and powdered hair, by Henry Bone (after Thomas Lawrence), August 1794</figcaption></figure>
<p>Grey was also noted for advocating Parliamentary reform and electoral reform. In 1792 he was the main force behind a petition submitted to Parliament in favor of reforms aimed at restoring "the freedom of election and a more equal representation of the people in parliament, and securing to the people a more frequent exercise of their right of electing their representatives," as a 1884 book described it.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The 1792 petition produced no change but in 1832 he pursued the goal more satisfactorily with passage of the Great Reform Bill. In his drive for fairer representation, he favored <a href="/wiki/Catholic_emancipation" title="Catholic emancipation">Catholic emancipation</a>. His affair with <a href="/wiki/Georgiana_Cavendish,_Duchess_of_Devonshire" title="Georgiana Cavendish, Duchess of Devonshire">Georgiana Cavendish, Duchess of Devonshire</a>, herself an active political campaigner, did him little harm although it nearly caused her to be divorced by her husband.
</p>
<h3><span id="Foreign_secretary.2C_1806.E2.80.931807"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_secretary,_1806–1807">Foreign secretary, 1806–1807</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Foreign secretary, 1806–1807">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1806, Grey, by then Lord Howick owing to his father's elevation to the peerage as <a href="/wiki/Earl_Grey" title="Earl Grey">Earl Grey</a>, became a part of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_All_the_Talents" title="Ministry of All the Talents">Ministry of All the Talents</a> (a coalition of <a href="/wiki/Foxite" title="Foxite">Foxite</a> Whigs, <a href="/wiki/William_Wyndham_Grenville,_1st_Baron_Grenville" class="mw-redirect" title="William Wyndham Grenville, 1st Baron Grenville">Grenvillites</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Henry_Addington,_1st_Viscount_Sidmouth" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry Addington, 1st Viscount Sidmouth">Addingtonites</a>) as <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Admiralty" title="First Lord of the Admiralty">First Lord of the Admiralty</a>.
</p><p>Following Fox's death later that year, Howick took over both as <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Secretary" title="Foreign Secretary">foreign secretary</a> and as leader of the Whigs. The ministry broke up in 1807 when George III blocked Catholic Emancipation legislation and required that all ministers individually sign a pledge, which Howick refused to do, that they would not "propose any further concessions to the Catholics".<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Years_in_opposition.2C_1807.E2.80.931830"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Years_in_opposition,_1807–1830">Years in opposition, 1807–1830</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Years in opposition, 1807–1830">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Charon%27s_Boat.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="A group of naked British Whig politicians, including three Grenvilles, Sheridan, St. Vincent, Moira, Temple, Erskine, Howick, Petty, Whitbread, Sheridan, Windham, and Tomline, Bishop of Lincoln, crossing the river Styx in a boat named the Broad Bottom Packet. Sidmouth&#39;s head emerges from the water next to the boat. The boat&#39;s torn sail has the inscription &quot;Catholic Emancipation&quot; and the centre mast is crowned with the Prince of Wales feathers and the motto &quot;Ich Dien&quot;. On the far side the shades of Cromwell, Charles Fox and Robespierre wave to them. Overhead, on brooms, are the Three Fates; to the left is a three-headed dog. Above the boat three birds soil the boat and politicians." src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Charon%27s_Boat.jpg/220px-Charon%27s_Boat.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Charon%27s_Boat.jpg/330px-Charon%27s_Boat.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Charon%27s_Boat.jpg/440px-Charon%27s_Boat.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4264" data-file-height="3044" /></a><figcaption>In <i>Charon's Boat</i> (1807), <a href="/wiki/James_Gillray" title="James Gillray">James Gillray</a> caricatured the fall of the Whig administration, with Howick taking the role of <a href="/wiki/Charon_(mythology)" class="mw-redirect" title="Charon (mythology)">Charon</a> rowing the boat.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The government fell from power the next year, and, after a brief period as a member of parliament for <a href="/wiki/Appleby_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Appleby (UK Parliament constituency)">Appleby</a> from May to July 1807, Howick went to the Lords, succeeding his father as Earl Grey. He continued in opposition for the next 23 years. There were times during this period when Grey came close to joining the Government. In 1811, <a href="/wiki/George_IV" title="George IV">the Prince Regent</a> tried to court Grey and his ally <a href="/wiki/William_Grenville,_1st_Baron_Grenville" title="William Grenville, 1st Baron Grenville">William Grenville</a> to join the <a href="/wiki/Spencer_Perceval" title="Spencer Perceval">Spencer Perceval</a> ministry following the resignation of <a href="/wiki/Richard_Wellesley,_1st_Marquess_Wellesley" title="Richard Wellesley, 1st Marquess Wellesley">Lord Wellesley</a>. Grey and Grenville declined because the Prince Regent refused to make concessions regarding Catholic Emancipation.<sup id="cite_ref-Smith_1996_paperback_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Smith_1996_paperback-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Grey's relationship with the Prince was strained further when his estranged daughter and heiress, <a href="/wiki/Princess_Charlotte_of_Wales_(1796%E2%80%931817)" title="Princess Charlotte of Wales (1796–1817)">Princess Charlotte</a>, turned to him for advice on how to avoid her father's choice of husband for her.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a>, Grey took the standard Whig party line. After being initially enthused by the Spanish uprising against Napoleon, Grey became convinced of the French emperor's invincibility following the defeat and death of Sir <a href="/wiki/John_Moore_(British_Army_officer)" title="John Moore (British Army officer)">John Moore</a>, the leader of the British forces in the <a href="/wiki/Peninsular_War" title="Peninsular War">Peninsular War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Grey was then slow to recognise the military successes of Moore's successor, the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington_Great_Duke" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Wellington Great Duke">Duke of Wellington</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> When Napoleon first abdicated in 1814, Grey objected to <a href="/wiki/Bourbon_Restoration_in_France" title="Bourbon Restoration in France">the restoration of the Bourbons</a>' authoritarian monarchy; and when Napoleon was reinstalled the following year, he said that the change was an internal French matter.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1826, believing that the Whig party no longer paid any attention to his opinions, Grey stood down as leader in favour of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Petty-Fitzmaurice,_3rd_Marquess_of_Lansdowne" title="Henry Petty-Fitzmaurice, 3rd Marquess of Lansdowne">Lord Lansdowne</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The following year, when <a href="/wiki/George_Canning" title="George Canning">George Canning</a> succeeded <a href="/wiki/Lord_Liverpool" class="mw-redirect" title="Lord Liverpool">Lord Liverpool</a> as prime minister, it was, therefore, Lansdowne and not Grey who was asked to join the Government, which needed strengthening following the resignations of <a href="/wiki/Robert_Peel" title="Robert Peel">Robert Peel</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Wellington">Duke of Wellington</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> When Wellington became prime minister in 1828, George IV (as the Prince Regent had become) singled out Grey as the one person he could not appoint to the Government.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_minister_.281830.E2.80.931834.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_minister_(1830–1834)">Prime minister (1830–1834)<span class="anchor" id="Premiership"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Prime minister (1830–1834)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Appointment">Appointment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Appointment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Whig_government,_1830%E2%80%931834" title="Whig government, 1830–1834">Whig government, 1830–1834</a></div>
<p>In 1830, following the death of George IV and when the <a href="/wiki/Duke_of_Wellington" class="mw-redirect" title="Duke of Wellington">Duke of Wellington</a> resigned on the question of Parliamentary reform, the Whigs finally returned to power, with Grey as prime minister. In 1831, he was made a member of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Garter" title="Order of the Garter">Order of the Garter</a>. His term was a notable one, seeing the passage of the <a href="/wiki/Reform_Act_1832" title="Reform Act 1832">Reform Act 1832</a>, which finally saw the reform of the House of Commons, and the <a href="/wiki/Abolitionism_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Abolitionism in the United Kingdom">abolition of slavery</a> throughout almost all of the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British Empire</a> in 1833 with the Slavery Abolition Act. As the years had passed, however, Grey had become more conservative, and he was cautious about initiating more far-reaching reforms, particularly since he knew that the King was at best only a reluctant supporter of reform.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Colonial_policy">Colonial policy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Colonial policy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Grey contributed to a plan to <a href="/wiki/British_colonisation_of_South_Australia" title="British colonisation of South Australia">found a new colony in South Australia</a>: in 1831 a "Proposal to His Majesty's Government for founding a colony on the Southern Coast of Australia" was prepared under the auspices of <a href="/wiki/Robert_Gouger" title="Robert Gouger">Robert Gouger</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Bacon_(British_Army_officer)" title="Anthony Bacon (British Army officer)">Anthony Bacon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jeremy_Bentham" title="Jeremy Bentham">Jeremy Bentham</a> and Grey, but its ideas were considered too radical, and it was unable to attract the required investment.<sup id="cite_ref-samem_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-samem-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In the same year, Grey was appointed to serve on the <a href="/wiki/Government_Commission_upon_Emigration" class="mw-redirect" title="Government Commission upon Emigration">Government Commission upon Emigration</a> (which was wound up in 1832).<sup id="cite_ref-stats1838_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stats1838-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Catholic_emancipation_and_retirement">Catholic emancipation and retirement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Catholic emancipation and retirement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>It was the issue of Ireland which precipitated the end of Grey's premiership in 1834. <a href="/wiki/Henry_Paget,_1st_Marquess_of_Anglesey" title="Henry Paget, 1st Marquess of Anglesey">Lord Anglesey</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Lord_Lieutenant_of_Ireland" title="Lord Lieutenant of Ireland">Lord Lieutenant of Ireland</a>, preferred conciliatory reform including the partial redistribution of the income from the <a href="/wiki/Tithes" class="mw-redirect" title="Tithes">tithes</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a> and away from the established <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Ireland" title="Church of Ireland">Church of Ireland</a>, a policy known as "appropriation".<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Chief_Secretary_for_Ireland" title="Chief Secretary for Ireland">Chief Secretary for Ireland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Edward_Geoffrey_Smith_Stanley,_14th_Earl_of_Derby" class="mw-redirect" title="Edward Geoffrey Smith Stanley, 14th Earl of Derby">Lord Stanley</a>, however, preferred coercive measures.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The cabinet was divided, and when <a href="/wiki/John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell" title="John Russell, 1st Earl Russell">Lord John Russell</a> drew attention in the House of Commons to their differences over appropriation, Stanley and others resigned.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> This triggered Grey to retire from public life, leaving <a href="/wiki/William_Lamb,_2nd_Viscount_Melbourne" title="William Lamb, 2nd Viscount Melbourne">Lord Melbourne</a> as his successor. Unlike most politicians, he seems to have genuinely preferred a private life; colleagues remarked caustically that he threatened to resign at every setback.
</p><p>Grey returned to Howick but kept a close eye on the policies of the new cabinet under Melbourne, whom he, and especially his family, regarded as a mere understudy until he began to act in ways of which they disapproved. Grey became more critical as the decade went on, being particularly inclined to see the hand of <a href="/wiki/Daniel_O%27Connell" title="Daniel O&#39;Connell">Daniel O'Connell</a> behind the scenes and blaming Melbourne for subservience to the <a href="/wiki/Radicalism_(historical)" class="mw-redirect" title="Radicalism (historical)">Radicals</a> with whom he identified the Irish patriot. He made no allowances for Melbourne's need to keep the radicals on his side to preserve his shrinking majority in the Commons, and in particular, he resented any slight on his own great achievement, the Reform Act, which he saw as a final solution of the question for the foreseeable future. He continually stressed its conservative nature. As he declared in his last great public speech, at the Grey Festival organised in his honour at <a href="/wiki/Edinburgh" title="Edinburgh">Edinburgh</a> in September 1834, its purpose was to strengthen and preserve the established constitution, to make it more acceptable to the people at large, and especially the middle classes, who had been the principal beneficiaries of the Reform Act, and to establish the principle that future changes would be gradual, "according to the increased intelligence of the people, and the necessities of the times".<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> It was the speech of a conservative statesman.<sup id="cite_ref-ODNB_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ODNB-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Lord_Grey.27s_ministry.2C_November_1830_.E2.80.93_July_1834"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Lord_Grey's_ministry,_November_1830_–_July_1834">Lord Grey's ministry, November 1830&#160;– July 1834</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Lord Grey&#039;s ministry, November 1830 – July 1834">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg/170px-Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg/255px-Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg/340px-Charles_Grey_-_2nd_Earl_Grey_-_atop_the_Grey_Momument_-_Newcastle_upon_Tyne_-_England_-_140804.jpg 2x" data-file-width="731" data-file-height="1009" /></a><figcaption>Lord Grey atop <a href="/wiki/Grey%27s_Monument" title="Grey&#39;s Monument">Grey's Monument</a>, looking down Grey Street in <a href="/wiki/Newcastle_upon_Tyne" title="Newcastle upon Tyne">Newcastle upon Tyne</a></figcaption></figure>
<ul><li>Lord Grey — <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Treasury" title="First Lord of the Treasury">First Lord of the Treasury</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_of_Lords" title="Leader of the House of Lords">Leader of the House of Lords</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_Brougham,_1st_Baron_Brougham_and_Vaux" title="Henry Brougham, 1st Baron Brougham and Vaux">Lord Brougham</a>&#160;— <a href="/wiki/Lord_Chancellor" title="Lord Chancellor">Lord Chancellor</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_Petty-FitzMaurice,_3rd_Marquess_of_Lansdowne" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry Petty-FitzMaurice, 3rd Marquess of Lansdowne">Lord Lansdowne</a> — <a href="/wiki/Lord_President_of_the_Council" title="Lord President of the Council">Lord President of the Council</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Lambton,_1st_Earl_of_Durham" title="John Lambton, 1st Earl of Durham">Lord Durham</a>&#160;— <a href="/wiki/Lord_Privy_Seal" title="Lord Privy Seal">Lord Privy Seal</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_Lamb,_2nd_Viscount_Melbourne" title="William Lamb, 2nd Viscount Melbourne">Lord Melbourne</a> — <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Home_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary of State for the Home Department">Secretary of State for the Home Department</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_John_Temple,_3rd_Viscount_Palmerston" title="Henry John Temple, 3rd Viscount Palmerston">Lord Palmerston</a>&#160;— <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_Foreign_Affairs_(UK)" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary of State for Foreign Affairs (UK)">Secretary of State for Foreign Affairs</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Frederick_John_Robinson,_1st_Viscount_Goderich" class="mw-redirect" title="Frederick John Robinson, 1st Viscount Goderich">Lord Goderich</a> — <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_War_and_the_Colonies" title="Secretary of State for War and the Colonies">Secretary of State for War and the Colonies</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/James_Robert_George_Graham" class="mw-redirect" title="James Robert George Graham">Sir James Graham</a> — <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Admiralty" title="First Lord of the Admiralty">First Lord of the Admiralty</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Charles_Spencer,_3rd_Earl_Spencer" class="mw-redirect" title="John Charles Spencer, 3rd Earl Spencer">Lord Althorp</a> — <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_of_Commons" title="Leader of the House of Commons">Leader of the House of Commons</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Grant,_1st_Baron_Glenelg" title="Charles Grant, 1st Baron Glenelg">Charles Grant</a> — <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Board_of_Control" title="President of the Board of Control">President of the Board of Control</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_Richard_Vassall-Fox,_3rd_Baron_Holland" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry Richard Vassall-Fox, 3rd Baron Holland">Lord Holland</a> — <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Duchy_of_Lancaster" title="Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster">Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Lennox,_5th_Duke_of_Richmond_and_Lennox" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles Lennox, 5th Duke of Richmond and Lennox">The Duke of Richmond</a> — <a href="/wiki/Postmaster_General_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Postmaster General of the United Kingdom">Postmaster General</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/George_Howard,_6th_Earl_of_Carlisle" title="George Howard, 6th Earl of Carlisle">Lord Carlisle</a> — <a href="/wiki/Minister_without_portfolio_(United_Kingdom)" title="Minister without portfolio (United Kingdom)">Minister without Portfolio</a></li></ul>
<p><b>Changes</b>
</p>
<ul><li>June 1831 — <a href="/wiki/Lord_John_Russell" class="mw-redirect" title="Lord John Russell">Lord John Russell</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Paymaster_of_the_Forces" title="Paymaster of the Forces">Paymaster of the Forces</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Edward_Smith-Stanley,_14th_Earl_of_Derby" title="Edward Smith-Stanley, 14th Earl of Derby">Edward Smith-Stanley</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chief_Secretary_for_Ireland" title="Chief Secretary for Ireland">Chief Secretary for Ireland</a>, join the Cabinet.</li>
<li>April 1833 — <a href="/wiki/Frederick_John_Robinson,_1st_Viscount_Goderich" class="mw-redirect" title="Frederick John Robinson, 1st Viscount Goderich">Lord Goderich</a>, now Lord Ripon, succeeds Lord Durham as Lord Privy Seal. Edward Smith-Stanley succeeds Ripon as <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_War_and_the_Colonies" title="Secretary of State for War and the Colonies">Secretary of State for War and the Colonies</a>. His successor as Chief Secretary for Ireland is not in the Cabinet. <a href="/wiki/Edward_Ellice,_Sr." class="mw-redirect" title="Edward Ellice, Sr.">Edward Ellice</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Secretary_at_War" title="Secretary at War">Secretary at War</a>, joins the Cabinet.</li>
<li>June 1834 — <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Spring_Rice,_1st_Baron_Monteagle_of_Brandon" title="Thomas Spring Rice, 1st Baron Monteagle of Brandon">Thomas Spring Rice</a> succeeds Stanley as Colonial Secretary. Lord Carlisle succeeds Ripon as Lord Privy Seal. <a href="/wiki/George_Eden,_1st_Earl_of_Auckland" title="George Eden, 1st Earl of Auckland">Lord Auckland</a> succeeds Graham as First Lord of the Admiralty. The Duke of Richmond leaves the Cabinet. His successor as Postmaster General is not in the Cabinet. <a href="/wiki/Charles_Poulett_Thomson" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles Poulett Thomson">Charles Poulett Thomson</a>, the <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Board_of_Trade" title="President of the Board of Trade">President of the Board of Trade</a>, and <a href="/wiki/James_Abercromby,_1st_Baron_Dunfermline" title="James Abercromby, 1st Baron Dunfermline">James Abercrombie</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Master_of_the_Mint" title="Master of the Mint">Master of the Mint</a>, join the Cabinet.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="231" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Lady-Caroline-Barrington-ne-Grey-Lady-Georgiana-Grey-Mary-Elizabeth-Grey-ne-Ponsonby-Countess-Grey_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="498" data-file-height="523" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Mary_Grey,_Countess_Grey" title="Mary Grey, Countess Grey">Mary Grey, Countess Grey</a> with her children Caroline and Georgiana</figcaption></figure>
<p>Before his marriage, Grey had an affair with the married <a href="/wiki/Georgiana_Cavendish,_Duchess_of_Devonshire" title="Georgiana Cavendish, Duchess of Devonshire">Georgiana Cavendish, Duchess of Devonshire</a>. Grey met Cavendish while attending a Whig society meeting in <a href="/wiki/Devonshire_House" title="Devonshire House">Devonshire House</a>, and they became lovers. In 1791 she became pregnant and was sent to France, where she gave birth to an <a href="/wiki/Illegitimate" class="mw-redirect" title="Illegitimate">illegitimate</a> daughter, who was raised by Grey's parents:<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Eliza_Courtney" title="Eliza Courtney">Eliza Courtney</a> (20 February 1792 – 2 May 1859<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>). She married <a href="/wiki/Robert_Ellice" title="Robert Ellice">Robert Ellice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage_and_legitimate_children">Marriage and legitimate children</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Marriage and legitimate children">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 18 November 1794, Grey married <a href="/wiki/Mary_Grey,_Countess_Grey" title="Mary Grey, Countess Grey">Mary Elizabeth Ponsonby</a> (1776–1861), only daughter of <a href="/wiki/William_Ponsonby,_1st_Baron_Ponsonby" title="William Ponsonby, 1st Baron Ponsonby">William Ponsonby, 1st Baron Ponsonby</a> of Imokilly and Louisa Molesworth. The marriage was a fruitful one; between 1796 and 1819 the couple had ten sons and six daughters:<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>unnamed daughter (stillborn, 1796)</li>
<li>Louisa Elizabeth Grey (7 April 1797 – 26 November 1841). She married <a href="/wiki/John_Lambton,_1st_Earl_of_Durham" title="John Lambton, 1st Earl of Durham">John Lambton, 1st Earl of Durham</a>, on 9 December 1816. They had five children, including Charles William, Grey's favourite grandson, who died young.</li>
<li>Elizabeth Grey (10 July 1798 – 8 November 1880). She married <a href="/wiki/John_Crocker_Bulteel" title="John Crocker Bulteel">John Crocker Bulteel</a> on 13 May 1826. They had five children.</li>
<li>Caroline Grey (30 August 1799 – 28 April 1875). She married Captain <a href="/wiki/George_Barrington_(Royal_Navy_officer)" title="George Barrington (Royal Navy officer)">George Barrington</a> on 15 January 1827. They had two children</li>
<li>Georgiana Grey (17 February 1801 – 13 September 1900), who never married.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_Grey,_3rd_Earl_Grey" title="Henry Grey, 3rd Earl Grey">Henry George Grey, 3rd Earl Grey</a> (28 December 1802 – 9 October 1894). He married Maria Copley on 9 August 1832.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Grey_(British_Army_officer)" title="Charles Grey (British Army officer)">General Charles Grey</a> (15 March 1804 – 31 March 1870). He married Caroline Farquhar on 26 July 1836. They had seven children, including <a href="/wiki/Albert_Grey,_4th_Earl_Grey" title="Albert Grey, 4th Earl Grey">Albert Grey, 4th Earl Grey</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Frederick_Grey" title="Frederick Grey">Admiral Sir Frederick William Grey</a> (23 August 1805 – 2 May 1878). He married Barbarina Sullivan on 20 July 1846.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mary_Wood,_Viscountess_Halifax" title="Mary Wood, Viscountess Halifax">Mary Grey</a> (2 May 1807 – 6 July 1884). She married <a href="/wiki/Charles_Wood,_1st_Viscount_Halifax" title="Charles Wood, 1st Viscount Halifax">Charles Wood, 1st Viscount Halifax</a>, on 29 July 1829. They had seven children.</li>
<li>William Grey (13 May 1808 – 11 February 1815), who died at the age of six.</li>
<li>Admiral George Grey (16 May 1809 – 3 October 1891). He married Jane Stuart (daughter of <a href="/wiki/Patrick_Stuart_(British_Army_general)" class="mw-redirect" title="Patrick Stuart (British Army general)">Patrick Stuart (British Army general)</a>) on 20 January 1845. They had eleven children.</li>
<li>Thomas Grey (29 December 1810 – 8 July 1826), who died at the age of fifteen.</li>
<li>Rev. John Grey MA, DD (2 March 1812 – 11 November 1895), Canon of Durham, Rector of Houghton-le-Spring. He married Lady Georgiana Hervey (daughter of <a href="/wiki/Frederick_Hervey,_1st_Marquess_of_Bristol" title="Frederick Hervey, 1st Marquess of Bristol">Frederick William Hervey, 1st Marquess of Bristol</a>) in July 1836. They had three children. He remarried Helen Spalding (maternal granddaughter of <a href="/wiki/John_Upton,_1st_Viscount_Templetown" title="John Upton, 1st Viscount Templetown">John Henry Upton, 1st Viscount Templetown</a>) on 11 April 1874.</li>
<li>Rev. Francis Richard Grey MA (31 March 1813 – 22 March 1890), Canon of Durham, Canon of Newcastle, Rector of Morpeth. He married Lady Elizabeth Howard, daughter of <a href="/wiki/George_Howard,_6th_Earl_of_Carlisle" title="George Howard, 6th Earl of Carlisle">George Howard, 6th Earl of Carlisle</a> on 12 August 1840.</li>
<li>Captain Henry Cavendish Grey (16 October 1814 – 5 September 1880)</li>
<li>William George Grey (15 February 1819 – 19 December 1865). He married Theresa Stedink on 20 September 1858.</li></ul>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg/220px-Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg/330px-Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg/440px-Inscription_Grey_Monument.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a><figcaption>Inscription on <a href="/wiki/Grey%27s_Monument" title="Grey&#39;s Monument">Grey's Monument</a></figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_years_and_death">Later years and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Later years and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg/220px-The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg/330px-The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg/440px-The_Grave_of_Earl_Grey_-_geograph.org.uk_-_385392.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a><figcaption>Grave at <a href="/wiki/Howick_Hall" title="Howick Hall">Howick Hall</a> in 	Howick, Northumberland</figcaption></figure>
<p>Grey spent his last years in contented, if sometimes fretful, retirement at Howick with his books, his family, and his dogs. The one great personal blow he suffered in old age was the death of his favourite grandson, Charles, at the age of 13. Grey became physically feeble in his last years and died quietly in his bed on 17 July 1845, forty-four years to the day since going to live at Howick.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> He was buried in the <a href="/wiki/St_Michael_and_All_Angels_Church_(Howick,_Northumberland)" title="St Michael and All Angels Church (Howick, Northumberland)">Church of St Michael and All Angels</a> there on the 26th in the presence of his family, close friends, and the labourers on his estate.<sup id="cite_ref-ODNB_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ODNB-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His biographer <a href="/wiki/G._M._Trevelyan" title="G. M. Trevelyan">G. M. Trevelyan</a> argues:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>in our domestic history 1832 is the next great landmark after <a href="/wiki/Glorious_Revolution" title="Glorious Revolution">1688</a>&#160;... [It] saved the land from revolution and civil strife and made possible the quiet progress of the <a href="/wiki/Victorian_era" title="Victorian era">Victorian era</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg/170px-Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="128" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg/255px-Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg/340px-Cup_of_Earl_Gray.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Earl_Grey_tea" title="Earl Grey tea">Earl Grey tea</a> is commonly believed to be named after Grey</figcaption></figure>
<p>Grey is commemorated by <a href="/wiki/Grey%27s_Monument" title="Grey&#39;s Monument">Grey's Monument</a> in the centre of <a href="/wiki/Newcastle_upon_Tyne" title="Newcastle upon Tyne">Newcastle upon Tyne</a>, which consists of a <a href="/wiki/Statue" title="Statue">statue</a> of Lord Grey standing atop a 40&#160;m (130&#160;ft) high <a href="/wiki/Column" title="Column">column</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The monument was damaged by lightning in 1941 and the statue's head was knocked off.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The monument lends its name to <a href="/wiki/Monument_Metro_station" title="Monument Metro station">Monument Metro station</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Tyne_and_Wear_Metro" title="Tyne and Wear Metro">Tyne and Wear Metro</a>, located directly underneath.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Grey_Street,_Newcastle" class="mw-redirect" title="Grey Street, Newcastle">Grey Street</a> in Newcastle upon Tyne, which runs south-east from the monument, is also named after Grey.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Durham_University" title="Durham University">Durham University</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Grey_College,_Durham" title="Grey College, Durham">Grey College</a> is named after Grey, who as prime minister in 1832 supported the Act of Parliament that established the university.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Earl_Grey_tea" title="Earl Grey tea">Earl Grey tea</a>, a blend which uses <a href="/wiki/Bergamot_essential_oil" title="Bergamot essential oil">bergamot oil</a> to flavour the brew, is commonly believed to be named after Grey, although the term was apparently first used decades after his death.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Charles_Grey,_2nd_Earl_Grey&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
